# 4.8.G.3 closing megaudit — W5-156 through W5-159

Audit the 4-commit arc closing the Phase 4.8.G.3 calcgraph hook wave:

  - 5adb7e13939 W5-156 — drop_table HIGH-1 (plan-cache flush) + HIGH-2 (bind-error → #NAME? in recompute_dirty + recompute_all)
  - b13e7c71ca1 W5-157 — on_table_rename hook + wire (re-keys table_to_formulas, substitutes Arc<str> in deps.tables)
  - 90ec3c481b2 W5-158 — on_column_rename hook + wire (coarse dirty-fan)
  - 3ff9c1501cc W5-159 — on_table_resize hook + wire + reextract_table_readers (refreshes range stripes via reextract_deps)

Background: the prior W5-155 commit added the on_table_drop wiring but a Codex audit found 2 HIGH issues (cache stale + bind error not mapped to cell). W5-156 closed both. W5-157-W5-159 extend the pattern to rename / column-rename / resize.

Focus areas:

1. **W5-156 HIGH-2 mapping correctness.** workbook_runtime.rs:3266 + :2882 map BindError::UnknownTable / UnknownTableColumn to Value::Error(Name) via put_computed_at. Is this complete? Are there other bind errors that should also map to #NAME? (UnresolvedName? UnknownSheet?)? The plan.rs docstring on UnknownTable explicitly says "mapped to Value::Error(#NAME?)" — does the docstring on related variants make analogous claims?

2. **W5-157 multi-table reference.** If a formula deps.tables = ["SALES", "ORDERS"] and we rename Sales→Costs, does the hook correctly update only the SALES entry and leave ORDERS alone? Walk the on_table_rename code at calcgraph_session.rs:982-1068.

3. **W5-159 reextract correctness.** workbook_runtime.rs:2331-2415 `reextract_table_readers`. Pattern mirrors `reextract_spill_footprint_readers`. Verify:
   - Borrow checker satisfied (alternating immutable/mutable).
   - PlanCacheKey construction matches the existing recompute path (text, sheet, name_gen, cell_anchor with @ awareness).
   - Bind failure marks dirty without re-extracting — does this leak stale formula_deps?
   - Order in resize_table: re-extract THEN hook. Is this correct, or should they swap?

4. **Resize-removes-column edge case.** If resize_table drops the last column, a formula referencing that column should produce #NAME? on next recompute. Walk the path: resize mutates metadata → reextract_table_readers tries to re-bind → bind fails (UnknownTableColumn) → mark_dirty + continue. Then on_table_resize fires (no-op since the formula's deps are stale and table still exists). Then recompute_dirty visits the dirty formula → W5-156 mapping produces #NAME?. Is this path correct?

5. **Stale state after bind failure in reextract_table_readers.** When the bind fails and we just mark_dirty without calling reextract_deps, the formula_deps stays with OLD table_to_formulas entries + OLD range_refs. Future table mutations would still touch this formula via table_to_formulas[OLD]. Is this acceptable, or should we clean up deps in the failure branch?

6. **Cross-cutting: 4.8.G.3 wave completeness.** Design § 14 row 7c lists 5 hooks: create / rename / drop / column-rename / resize. We shipped 4 (rename, drop, column-rename, resize). on_table_create was deferred (depends on 4.8.N soft-fail). Is this defensible, or does it leave a gap?

7. **Stale docstrings or comments anywhere** referencing "4.8.G.3 deferred" / "plan_cache.clear() brute force" / etc. that should be updated now that the wave is complete.

Report findings as HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW with file:line, contract violated, and proposed fix. Be terse but specific. If you disagree with the W5-156 / W5-159 design choices, say so plainly.

     1	//! `ExprPlan` — execution-ready intermediate representation.
     2	//!
     3	//! Sits between `ql_formula_syntax::Expr` (parser output, may carry `Option<SheetId>` for
     4	//! unresolved same-sheet refs) and the kernel dispatch in `scalar.rs` / `simd.rs` (W4-2).
     5	//! `bind()` walks the Expr tree and resolves every `CellAddr.sheet: Option<SheetId>` to a
     6	//! concrete `SheetId` based on the formula's owning sheet.
     7	//!
     8	//! The plan is "execution-ready" but still tree-shaped — it does NOT yet flatten the
     9	//! expression into a SIMD-friendly opcode stream. That's the role of `kernel_simd` in W4-2,
    10	//! which lowers `ExprPlan::Binary { op: Mul, lhs: CellRef, rhs: Number }` into a
    11	//! `multiversion`-dispatched `Float64Array * f64` call.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Phase 0 scope
    14	//!
    15	//! Subset of `Expr` variants covered:
    16	//! - `Number(f64)` — numeric literal
    17	//! - `Bool(bool)` — boolean literal
    18	//! - `String(Arc<str>)` — text literal (binds 1:1)
    19	//! - `CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` — sheet RESOLVED to concrete SheetId
    20	//! - `Binary { op, lhs, rhs }` — arithmetic + concat + comparison
    21	//! - `Unary { op, operand }` — unary minus / plus / percent
    22	//!
    23	//! Covered through Phase 4.7:
    24	//! - `RangeRef` — single range used inside a Function call (e.g. `SUM(A:A)`)

    90	    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array literal lowered from `Expr::Array`.
    91	    /// Row-major; outer `Vec` is rows, inner `Vec` is cells. Element-arity

   138	        table_name: Arc<str>,
   139	        /// Original parsed spec subtree (for printer / diagnostics).
   140	        source: Arc<ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree>,
   141	        /// Resolved range. For column refs: the column's data range.
   142	        /// For `#Headers`: the header row's columns. For `#All`: the
   143	        /// full footprint. For `[@Col]`: the column's FULL data range
   144	        /// (eval narrows to current row).
   145	        resolved: Range,
   146	        /// `true` iff this is a `[@Col]` / `[[#This Row], [Col]]` form
   147	        /// that requires cell-time row narrowing.
   148	        is_this_row: bool,
   149	    },
   150	}

  2328	    /// **W5-159 (Phase 4.8.G.3):** re-bind every formula registered

  2332	    ///
  2333	    /// Pattern mirrors `reextract_spill_footprint_readers` (W5-103):
  2334	    /// step 1 collects readers under an immutable graph borrow; step 2
  2335	    /// re-binds + re-extracts under alternating immutable workbook /
  2336	    /// mutable graph borrows. A bind failure (e.g., a column was
  2337	    /// removed by the resize) marks the reader dirty so
  2338	    /// `recompute_dirty`'s W5-156 bind-error mapping produces `#NAME?`
  2339	    /// at the cell.
  2340	    fn reextract_table_readers(&mut self, table_canonical: &str) {
  2341	        // Step 1: collect (node, sheet, row, col, text) under an
  2342	        // immutable borrow of the graph + workbook.
  2343	        let reader_info: Vec<(ql_calcgraph::NodeId, SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = {
  2344	            let g = match self.graph.as_deref() {
  2345	                Some(g) => g,
  2346	                None => return,
  2347	            };
  2348	            g.dependents_for_table(table_canonical)
  2349	                .into_iter()
  2350	                .filter_map(|n| {
  2351	                    let (s, r, c) = g.cell_address_for(n)?;
  2352	                    let text = self.workbook.formula_at(s, r, c).cloned()?;
  2353	                    Some((n, s, r, c, text))
  2354	                })
  2355	                .collect()
  2356	        };
  2357	
  2358	        // Step 2: re-bind + re-extract.
  2359	        for (node, sheet, row, col, text) in reader_info {
  2360	            let name_gen = self.workbook.names().generation();
  2361	            let cell_anchor = if text.contains('@') {
  2362	                Some((row, col))
  2363	            } else {
  2364	                None
  2365	            };
  2366	            let cache_key = PlanCacheKey {
  2367	                text: Arc::clone(&text),
  2368	                sheet,
  2369	                name_gen,
  2370	                cell_anchor,
  2371	            };

  2373	                .plan_cache

  2385	                        self.workbook,
  2386	                        self.workbook,
  2387	                        self.workbook,
  2388	                    )?)

  2877	    /// Begin a multi-cell transaction. The returned `WorkbookTransaction`
  2878	    /// borrows the runtime's workbook + registry for its lifetime. Buffer
  2879	    /// writes via `put_value`/`put_formula` then call `commit` to apply them
  2880	    /// atomically. See `transaction::WorkbookTransaction` for full semantics.
  2881	    ///
  2882	    /// Phase 2A.3.b (2026-05-12): if the runtime was constructed with
  2883	    /// `with_oplog`, the transaction inherits the op-log handle via
  2884	    /// `Option::as_deref_mut` (re-borrowed for the transaction's shorter
  2885	    /// lifetime). The runtime is borrow-frozen while the transaction is
  2886	    /// alive, so a single op log records both individual edits and batched
  2887	    /// commits without aliasing.
  2888	    pub fn transaction(&mut self) -> WorkbookTransaction<'_> {
  2889	        WorkbookTransaction::with_optional_oplog(
  2890	            self.workbook,
  2891	            self.registry,
  2892	            self.oplog.as_deref_mut(),
  2893	        )
  2894	    }

  2897	    /// refresh stale values (the qbook loader stores formula text + a sentinel
  2898	    /// value; this method computes the real value).

  2900	    /// Iteration order is HashMap-arbitrary, so cross-cell dependencies may
  2901	    /// evaluate in a non-deterministic order. Engine Phase 3 calcgraph
  2902	    /// integration will add topological scheduling for deterministic + correct
  2903	    /// dependency resolution (see `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` Phase 3.4; tracked as
  2904	    /// GAP-R-01 in `docs/known-gaps.md`).
  2905	    ///

  2907	    /// RuntimeError>` to `RecomputeResult` (always returns; no Result

  2909	    /// dropped per-cell context; the new shape continues past failures

  2911	    ///
  2912	    /// Cells that fail structurally (lex/parse/bind) keep their

  2919	    /// on-keystroke diagnostics. Runs the full lex → parse → bind → eval

  2928	    ///

  2930	    /// in the formula bar, without committing the formula until Enter.
  2931	    /// Each keystroke can call `validate_formula(sheet, row, col, draft)`

  2946	        // two-tier sheet-then-workbook scope chain fires.

  2948	        // structured-ref `[@Col]` validation works.

  2952	            self.workbook,

  2963	        // give per the scalar-context contract at scalar.rs:95).

  2966	        // made the IDE preview inconsistent with `set_formula`'s actual
  2967	        // spill behavior. With this change, validate and set_formula

  2969	        // the SHAPE of the spill from validate; that would need a
  2970	        // dedicated `ValidationResult` enum (deferred — see GAP-I-04).

  2976	            // surface as `#CALC!` per the existing scalar-context
  2977	            // contract (preserved via `into_scalar_for_test`-style
  2978	            // logic inlined here to avoid the cfg(test) gate).
  2979	            crate::eval_result::EvalResult::Array(a) if a.is_degenerate() => {
  2980	                Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)

  2982	            crate::eval_result::EvalResult::Array(a) => a.at(0, 0).clone(),
  2983	        })

docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:26:## 2. Non-goals (deferred)
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:177:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:181:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:182:| 8 | **4.9.H** (W5-144, shipped, binder-only — no ExprPlan variant) | Binder narrows `@expr` at BIND TIME per design § 3.3 (rules 1-9). Strategy refined during implementation: NO `ExprPlan::ImplicitIntersection` variant — every reachable case collapses to an existing ExprPlan shape, so the wrapper is dropped at bind. Mapping: rule 1 (scalar) → pass-through; rule 5 (single cell / `A1:A1`) → CellRef; rules 2/3 (single col/row range with anchor in span) → narrowed CellRef; rule 4 (2-D range) → `ExprPlan::Error(#VALUE!)`; rule 6 (WholeColumn/WholeRow) → narrowed CellRef (multi-col `A:C` → `#VALUE!`); rule 7 (array literal) → top-left literal; rule 8 (function) → bind inner as-is (scalar return passes through; array return is `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit, known gap); rule 9 (StructuredRef) → bind via `resolve_structured_ref` then patch `is_this_row: true` so eval narrows to formula row (makes `@Sales[Qty]` ≡ `Sales[@Qty]`). Nested `@@expr` collapses at bind via recursion. New `BindError::ImplicitIntersectionRequiresAnchor` fires for range narrowing without `site.cell`. **AST round-trip preserved** — printer still emits `@<inner>` per W5-143; only the eval plan drops the wrapper. **Known gaps** documented for cell-boundary spill rewiring (4.9.L): rule 7 for function-returned arrays, full rule-8 narrowing of array-returning functions, named-range narrowing for `Range`-resolving names. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:186:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:188:| 14 | **4.9.N** (W5-152, shipped) | Polish + deferred-style edge cases from Sonnet's audit (closing-megaudit LOW list). Closed: LOW-1 stale `Expr::Spill` docstrings in `printer.rs` + `plan.rs` (the pre-W5-152 text claimed Phase 4.9 would add `A1#` syntax; updated to "a future Phase"); LOW-2 printer's `axis_spec_to_absolute_coord` bound tightened from `u32::MAX` to `MAX_ROW` / `MAX_COLUMN` with axis-specific param threaded through 6 callers (defense-in-depth — production paths can't reach the lax bound but a directly-constructed AST could); LOW-3 per-axis-independent R1C1 range relativity test pin (`R1C[1]:R10C[5]` and `R[1]C1:R[5]C10` shapes that the W5-139 parser accepts but had no test). LOW-4 (`mem::take` fragility in `parse_r1c1_axis`) NOT FIXED — cosmetic per Sonnet; defensive refactor risks introducing a real bug. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:189:| 15 | **4.9.O** (W5-150 + W5-151, shipped) | Closing megaudit. **Three-way audit convergence:** self-review (Opus), Sonnet (via Agent), AND Codex (via `codex exec`) all independently identified the SAME **HIGH-1: PlanCache × `@<range>` cell collision**. Codex's findings arrived later than the shell-exit notification suggested — the retry transcript at `docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md` ends with a clean triage + verdict `NOT SHIP-READY` (against the pre-W5-150 scope). Sonnet additionally surfaced **MEDIUM-1** (@-StructuredRef over-broad `is_this_row=true` patch; broke single-cell-special-form idempotency and 2-D error class) + **MEDIUM-2** (`ReferenceModeWire` missing `Unknown(_)` forward-compat variant; design § 4.9.J promised but code didn't deliver). **Closures:** HIGH-1 at W5-150 (`cell_anchor: Option<(RowId, ColId)>` added to `PlanCacheKey`, conditional on `@` in canonical text); MEDIUM-1 at W5-151 (override `is_this_row` only when spec is `BareColumn`); MEDIUM-2 at W5-151 (mirror `LocaleWire` pattern — custom Serialize/Deserialize + `ReplayError::UnknownReferenceMode`). **Verdict: SHIP-READY triple-confirmed.** Full audit transcripts at `docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-{,codex,codex-retry,sonnet}.md`. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:201:| Q5 Locale data source | Hardcoded en/de/fr in Rust. CLDR deferred to known-gap doc. Codex + Sonnet agree. |
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:19:That document (dated 2026-05-10, 949 lines) is the **product source-of-truth**: the fusion thesis, the wedge, the v1 ship definition, the 11-phase / 9-12 month budget, the kill gates, the v1.5 deferrals, the legal posture, the renderer/Python/IDE phase plan. It is gitignored (lives outside this engine worktree). Future sessions should read it before making product-level decisions.
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:34:| **Engine 5 (Collaboration CRDT)** | Product Phase 9 (deferred to v1.5 per canonical plan) | Canonical plan defers full collab to v1.5; engine-side, when this work happens, it lives here. May be cut from v1 per canonical. |
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:69:Phase 2A closed a large correctness and persistence pass: defined names, transactions, load-and-recompute, dotted function identifiers, schema v2, crash-safe save, Excel-canon coercion fixes, deterministic formula fingerprints, error ergonomics, and audit closure. Phase 2A.3 then shipped Loro-backed op-log scaffolding: `ql-oplog`, typed `Op` variants, Loro snapshot persistence, replay, runtime and transaction producer wiring, and `.qbook/oplog.bin` persistence. The stale `docs/phase2/entry-plan.md` and `docs/phase2/exit-packet.md` still say 2A.3 is deferred; this plan supersedes that statement and requires a doc-rot repair pass in Phase 2B.
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:135:- `docs/phase2/*` are known stale; do not trust their 2A.3 deferral statement.
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:164:   Bind `SUM(Sales)` to a typed `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { name, range }` instead of generic `UnsupportedVariant`; scalar-context misuse surfaces precise `BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`. Evaluation returns `#CALC!` for AggregateNameRef pending Phase 3.6. **Deviation from original plan:** the work stays in `ql-exec/src/plan.rs` rather than moving to `ql-formula-semantics`. The move was deferred because `ExprPlan` lives in `ql-exec`; relocating it cleanly requires Phase 3 calcgraph integration to land first (so the eventual home is `ql-formula-semantics` once ownership questions resolve). Tracked as a follow-up; not a 2B.4 blocker.  
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:182:   Dispatch independent audit after 2B.2-2B.6. Fix HIGH findings, triage MEDIUM, document deferrals.  
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:314:   Notes: RANDARRAY / INDIRECT / OFFSET / INFO / CELL still deferred to Phase 4.3 (function library expansion). NOW/TODAY date arithmetic is approximate (Excel 1900 leap-year quirk not modeled); Phase 4.5 (date/time + format) pins exact semantics.  
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:383:- **GAP-G-01 + GAP-G-03 SHIPPED (W5-50, 2026-05-13).** Decision recorded in `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md` (W5-49) and implemented W5-50: Option A — `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` + `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula` backed by `formula_to_stripe_keys` reverse index, wired into `extract_and_register_deps`. Option C — `topo::schedule_with_supplemental` accepts a HashMap of temp adjacency; `CalcgraphSession::build_range_supplemental` constructs F→G edges for dirty-formula pairs inside ranges. FormulaRegion binder (Option B) still deferred to Phase 4.7. Phase 4.3 V2 (SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup family) is **now unblocked**. Tests: +35 vs Phase 3 exit (981 → 1016 workspace).

docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:429:   FN4-01 closure complete (W5-58). Shipped: trig W5-51, SUMIF/COUNTIF W5-53, lookup family W5-54, IFS family + SUMPRODUCT W5-55, text wave 2 W5-56, math completion + hyperbolic W5-57, stats family W5-58. **Phase 4.3 polish wave 1 shipped W5-61 + closed W5-62**: wildcards in SUMIF/COUNTIF/SUMIFS/AVERAGEIF/SEARCH (`?`, `*`, `~` escape; text-cell-only), CONCAT (range-aware, 32K cap), PROPER, CLEAN, CEILING.MATH, FLOOR.MATH, RANK.AVG. Polish remaining (deferred to wave 2 / Phase 4.7+): MODE.MULT (needs spill), CEILING.PRECISE / FLOOR.PRECISE, FN4-03 lazy IF/IFERROR.

docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md:77:| `on_set_name(name)` | Look up `name_to_formulas[name]` (case-insensitive); mark all referencing formulas dirty. Today only `AggregateNameRef` populates this index — scalar `NameRef` precision is GAP-R-07, deferred to Phase 4. |
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-format-string-grammar.md:311:| Elapsed time `[h]` | parsed + rendered | rejected | tier-deferred; useful but adds renderer complexity |
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-format-string-grammar.md:312:| Fraction `# ?/?` | parsed + rendered | rejected | low frequency in real workbooks; defer |
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:52:- **Binder**: resolves `SheetRef::Name → Id` against the current sheet registry. Unknown name → `BindError::UnknownSheet`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:133:- `Sheet1!Sheet2!A1` — INVALID; cross-sheet refs are flat in Excel. Parser emits `BindError::DoubleSheetQualifier`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:137:- Reserved sheet names (`AI`, future): treated as plain SheetName at lex time; binder rejects with `BindError::ReservedSheetName` if needed.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:191:- `Name(s)` → `sheets.resolve(s)` or `BindError::UnknownSheet { name }`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:194:**Implication:** parser stays pure-text + workbook-free. IDE syntax-highlight uses `parse(text)` exactly as today. Binder is the single point of sheet-name resolution. `BindError::UnknownSheet` is the diagnostic surface for unknown-name syntax.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:202:`bind_with_names` already produces `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet: SheetId, .. }`. The only change: cross-sheet refs that resolve to a sheet that no longer exists by bind time (between parse and bind, the sheet was deleted) need a clean error. Add `BindError::SheetDeletedAfterParse { sheet_id }`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:219:- **B — single NameTable with scope field:** `NameTable { entries: HashMap<NameKey, NamedTarget>, .. }` where `NameKey = (Option<SheetId>, CanonicalName)`. Lookups try `(Some(sheet), name)` first, fall back to `(None, name)`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:263:- `ReplayError::SheetRenameUnknownSheet { id }` — `RenameSheet` references a sheet that wasn't created by earlier ops.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:264:- `ReplayError::SheetScopedNameUnknownSheet { id, name }` — same for sheet-scoped names.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:295:- New `ParseError::UnknownSheet`, `DanglingBang`, `DoubleSheetQualifier`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:353:## 11. Non-goals (explicitly deferred)
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:355:- **3D references** (`Sheet1:Sheet5!A1`) — Excel-canon but rarely used; defer to Phase 4.7 or post-v1.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:396:| HIGH-4 | cross-sheet range grammar under-specified | § 10.5 added — range prefix rules, mixed-endpoint rejection, 3D deferral |
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:406:| LOW-1 | `BindError::SheetDeletedAfterParse` unreachable | § 6.1 revised — moved to runtime-read `#REF!` |
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:12:- **HIGH (§ 6.3):** "scalar context returns first cell" was not Excel canon. **Revised:** v1 returns `#CALC!` for array-in-scalar-context; proper implicit intersection / `@` was deferred to Phase 4.9 and SHIPPED there (W5-138 → W5-152). The array-in-`@`-scalar-context narrowing (design § 3.3 rule 7 — `@TRANSPOSE(...)` → top-left) remained deferred at Phase 4.9 close as a known gap; cell-boundary spill rewiring (a future Phase) closes it.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:31:- **AST:** `Expr::Array(Vec<Vec<Expr>>)` shape-locked in `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/ast.rs:63`. `Expr::Spill(Box<Expr>)` shape-locked at line 67 but is RESERVED for Excel's `A1#` spill-range-ref syntax (Phase 4.9), NOT for the runtime spill anchor. Parser doesn't construct either; binder rejects with `BindError::UnsupportedVariant`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:122:BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected: u32, found: u32, row: u32 }
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:125:This shadows the equivalent `ParseError` for the case where the AST was constructed directly. No `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` for `Expr::Array` itself — it's now supported.

docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:513:**Decision (post-W5-108 / Phase 4.7.O closure update):** Option b shipped. `Graph::readers_in_rect` (used by `WorkbookRuntime::reextract_spill_footprint_readers` at `workbook_runtime.rs:842-957`) walks ONLY readers indexed under the OLD or NEW footprint and re-extracts their deps. The original design proposed Option a for v1 ("matches the existing `name_gen` invalidation cadence at edit-rate"); 4.7.J HIGH-1 closure shipped the targeted variant directly. The big-hammer Option a remains a valid simplification if the reverse map proves too clever in practice.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:527:| 6 | **4.7.F** (W5-100) | Binder: `Expr::Array` → `ExprPlan::Array` lowering + `BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch` (NOT panic) | Self + Sonnet |
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:540:**Stop conditions** (defer remaining to a Phase 4.7 polish wave):
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:602:**v1 scope (post-W5-108 / Phase 4.7.O closure update):** 1D inputs only — `array` and `include` must both be row vectors (1×N) or both column vectors (N×1), with matching length. 2D inputs (N×M array + N×1 or 1×M mask) deferred to v2 / Phase 4.10. The shipped implementation at `crates/ql-functions/src/array_returning_fns.rs:335-341` rejects any `array` with `rows > 1 AND cols > 1` with `#VALUE!`. The 2D enumeration below describes the eventual target shape; do NOT take it as the shipped 4.7 scope.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:702:   **A:** Constants + error literals only in v1. Refs deferred to Phase 4.10 (§ 3.3).
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:7:> **STALE BANNER (added 2026-05-12 end-of-day):** This document was written when Phase 2A.3 (op log) was still deferred. Since then, 2A.3 has shipped in three commits: `dfc2a9900ef` (scaffolding) + `a6de4b4a50b` (runtime/transaction wiring) + `fc977815cd2` (`oplog.bin` persistence). The canonical engine plan is now `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md`. New sessions: read MASTER-PLAN.md first. This file remains for historical context of the Phase 2A roadmap.
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:107:**Closed audit H2's deferred path.** Bare identifiers (4+ letter) now parse to `Expr::NameRef(name)` and resolve through the workbook's `NameTable` at bind time. Unresolved names surface as `BindError::UnresolvedName`. See commit message for Phase 2A.1 / W5-11.
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:112:3. `BindError::UnresolvedName(Arc<str>)` variant added.
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:117:8. Tests added: 7 runtime tests (Cell / Number / Boolean / Text / unresolved → `UnresolvedName` / Range → `UnsupportedVariant` / mixed-case canonicalization), 2 parser tests (`parse_bare_identifier_*_to_name_ref`).
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:142:- **Eager validation**: `put_formula` runs lex + parse + bind at call time. Errors (syntax, `UnresolvedName`, `UnsupportedVariant`) surface BEFORE any state change.
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:189:**Tests** (6 in `loader::tests`): formula refresh against stale-on-disk values, formula-free workbook, missing-path load error, empty workbook, multiple formulas all recomputed, named-range round-trip pins the persistence gap (`.qbook` doesn't yet serialize `NameTable`; load surfaces `Recompute(UnresolvedName)` — flagged for Phase 2B+).
docs/phase2/entry-plan.md:292:6. On agent return: fix HIGH findings in one focused commit, defer MEDIUM/LOW where appropriate.

docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:111:CONCERN-I in the brief flagged this. Each `SUMIF(A:A, "foo*", B:B)` recompiles `WildcardPattern` on every formula evaluation. The pattern is invariant of the data, so a per-formula compile + cache (keyed on the criteria text) would help. This was filed as deferred-perf by the brief; I confirm it's worth a GAP-F entry rather than silent deferral.
docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:228:**One LOW, one LOW.** L1 (per-call Vec<char> allocation) and L2 (per-call pattern compile). Both are real, both are deferrable, both should be filed as GAP-F entries if not already.
docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:276:8. **[L1, L2] File GAP-F-XX entries for the two perf-deferred items** — per-call `to_uppercase + collect` allocation and per-call pattern compile.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:20:5. **Mutation API** — `WorkbookRuntime::create_table`, `rename_table`, `rename_column`, `resize_table`, `drop_table`. Each emits a corresponding op-log entry.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:24:## 2. Non-goals (deferred)
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:27:- **3D table refs** (across sheets). Excel canonical syntax is fuzzy here; defer.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:158:5. **Tables block ALL spill anchors inside their footprint** — REVISED post-Codex (HIGH-3). Original draft allowed within-table spills if they fit; Excel canon DOES NOT allow array formulas inside Tables. `=SEQUENCE(3)` typed into ANY cell inside a table footprint produces `#SPILL!` at the anchor (even if the resulting array would fit). Scalar formulas inside tables remain allowed. Validation runs at THREE entry points (W5-124): (a) `write_spill` consults `Workbook::table_at(anchor)` before registering — blocks the "type array formula INSIDE existing table" path; (b) `create_table` walks the proposed footprint checking `Workbook::spill_anchor_at` — blocks the "create table OVER existing anchor" path; (c) `resize_table` walks NEWLY claimed cells with the same check — blocks the "grow table TO COVER existing anchor" path. Replay arms mirror (b) + (c).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:160:6. **Table footprint fits within the Excel address grid** — NEW W5-125 (Phase 4.8.O.1 closure, Codex HIGH-1). `top_row + rows - 1` must be `≤ MAX_ROW` (1,048,575) and `top_col + cols - 1` must be `≤ MAX_COLUMN` (16,383). `TableMetadata::validate_footprint_bounds` (in `ql-storage`) is the shared check; called at FIVE entry points: producer `create_table` / `resize_table`, replay `apply_create_table` / `apply_resize_table`, and v6 `.qbook` loader. Pre-W5-125 the check was missing, so `for r in top_row..top_row + rows` either ran past the addressable grid (registering cells later `Sheet::put` would panic on) or u32-overflowed silently (skipping all downstream overlap / spill-anchor checks).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:164:Decision: use `Arc<str>` (uppercase canonical) as the primary key, matching `NameTable`. A separate `TableId(u32)` is tempting for the graph (stable across renames) but adds complexity. **Renames REWRITE formula text** (Excel canon, see § 12.2). The plan-cache-invalidation alternative (the original draft's approach) was broken: cached plans rebind against the OLD name and fail `BindError::UnknownTable`, breaking bind/recompute/persistence. Rewrite-on-rename is the simpler correct path.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:170:Mirrors `name_to_formulas` (existing in `CalcgraphSession`). Every formula's `extract_and_register_deps` pass discovers `StructuredRef` references and registers them in `table_to_formulas: HashMap<Arc<str>, HashSet<NodeId>>`. The runtime fires `on_table_create / on_table_rename / on_column_rename / on_table_resize / on_table_drop` hooks against this index when table metadata mutates. These hooks dirty the indexed formulas + transitively propagate dirty downstream (matching the W5-91 / `on_set_name` BFS fanout pattern). Without this index, `resize_table` growth would NOT dirty formulas reading the new rows.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:231:After lexing an Ident, peek for `[`. If present, consume the bracket-balanced content (per § 5.4 escape rules) and emit `Token::StructuredRef`. **No function-name exclusion at the lexer level**: `SUM[Qty]` lexes as a structured ref (functions distinguish by `(`, not `[`); table-name validity is enforced by `create_table` (§ 12.1 + § 5.3).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:237:Excel table-name rules (enforced at `create_table` validation, NOT at the lexer):
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:246:`A[0]` will lex as `Token::StructuredRef { table_name: "A", bracket_content: "0" }`. `create_table("A", ...)` will REJECT the name. So `A[0]` would emit `BindError::UnknownTable("A")` — which is acceptable: no table named `A` can exist. The lexer doesn't reject; the binder surfaces a precise error.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:356:1. **Lookup** the table in `Workbook::tables` by case-insensitive `table_name`. Missing → `BindError::UnknownTable(table_name)`.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:358:   - `Column(col)` → look up column index by `col`. Missing → `BindError::UnknownTableColumn { table, col }`. Resolve to the column's data range.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:365:   - `ThisRowColumn(col)` — requires bind context to know the formula's cell address. If the formula's cell IS inside the table's data region, resolve to the single cell at `(formula_row, col_offset)`. Otherwise → `BindError::ThisRowOutsideTable`.

docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:419:Implementation: introduce a `BindError::is_table_related(&self) -> bool` and route table-related bind errors through a soft-fail path in `set_formula`. Recompute path naturally re-binds via plan cache invalidation when the table appears, so `on_table_create` dirties the formula and the next eval succeeds.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:423:The original draft proposed a `table_gen: u64` field in `PlanCacheKey`. Per HIGH-1 closure (§ 12.2 — formula text is rewritten on rename), `table_gen` is no longer needed for renames. It IS needed for non-rename mutations: `resize_table` (range changes; cached plans hold the OLD resolved range), `column_rename` (column index changes), `drop_table` (cached plans hold a now-invalid range). Add `table_gen` for these cases:
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:426:struct PlanCacheKey {
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:558:- **v5 reader loading v6 file**: the existing reader bound `v4..=WORKBOOK_SCHEMA_VERSION` REFUSES v6. **This is correct**: silently dropping table metadata would break formulas (a formula referencing `Sales[Qty]` would surface `BindError::UnknownTable` after load → cell renders `#NAME?` when pre-save it was a valid SUM). Loud refusal preserves semantic integrity.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:579:### 12.1 `WorkbookRuntime::create_table`
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:582:pub fn create_table(
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:606:### 12.2 `rename_table` / `rename_column` — REVISED post-Codex (HIGH-1)
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:608:**Formula text IS rewritten on rename** (Excel canon). The original draft proposed plan-cache invalidation + no rewrite — Codex correctly noted that cached plans rebind against the OLD name and fail `BindError::UnknownTable`, breaking bind/recompute/persistence.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:610:**Implementation flow** for `rename_table(old, new)`:
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:620:`rename_column` follows the same pattern but rewrites column references INSIDE bracket content rather than the table name itself.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:624:**Cells with formula text referencing tables that didn't exist at bind time** are listed in a `pending_table_refs: HashSet<(Arc<str>, NodeId)>` map (populated when bind surfaces `UnknownTable`). `on_table_create` queries this map + dirties the matching formulas so the next eval re-binds with the new table in scope.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:626:### 12.3 `resize_table` — REVISED post-Codex (LOW-3, HIGH-2)
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:633:Inserting / removing a column in the MIDDLE is NOT supported in 4.8 (deferred to Phase 5 structural edits — requires physical cell move).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:637:### 12.4 `drop_table`
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:639:Removes metadata. Formula text referencing the dropped table re-binds to `BindError::UnknownTable` → emits `#NAME?` (matching defined-name removal precedent).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:643:1. **No fallbacks** — unknown table / column / specifier surfaces a specific bind error (per CLAUDE.md "errors must be visible"). Table-related bind errors map to cell error values per § 7.4 (so a formula referencing a not-yet-created table is accepted with an error cell value; later `create_table` dirties + re-binds).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:646:4. **Plan cache key extension** — `table_gen` joins `name_gen` and `sheet` in `PlanCacheKey` (only for resize/column_rename/drop; create+rename rewrite formula text so text-keyed cache misses anyway).
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:649:7. **`[@Col]` requires owning_cell context** — bind without context fails precisely with `BindError::ThisRowRequiresOwningCell`.

docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:712:1. **4.8.G.3 calcgraph hooks** — optional perf optimization. Current rename/resize use brute-force `plan_cache.clear()` + caller-driven `recompute_all`; `table_to_formulas` reverse index + `on_table_*` hooks would automate the "caller-must-recompute" contract. Becomes valuable when tables grow large or mutation rate is high.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:713:2. **4.8.N soft-fail** — backwards-incompatible API contract change. Currently `set_formula("SUM(Sales[Qty])")` BEFORE `create_table("Sales", ...)` hard-rejects with `BindError::UnknownTable`. Design § 7.4 wants accept-text-and-surface-error-cell-value with re-bind on `on_table_create`. Needs explicit sign-off on the contract change before implementation.

docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.G-sonnet-review.md:311:   Add a brief comment that `SUMPRODUCT({...}, {...})` is currently `#CALC!` because SUMPRODUCT is range-aware (not scalar-aggregate), and its array expansion is deferred to Phase 4.9 alongside SUMPRODUCT's unified-ABI migration.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:104:**Test hooks:** `set_test_now_secs(unix_secs)` continues to override `unix_secs`. New `set_test_utc_offset_seconds(offset)` (default 0 — UTC == local for deterministic tests) overrides the offset. In production, the default `NowProvider` implementation reads from `chrono::Local::now().offset()` OR — to avoid the `chrono` dep — calls into a small `iana_time_zone` lookup (~50 LOC dep) OR uses the libc `localtime_r` FFI. **Decision deferred to 4.5.A implementation:** prototype both and benchmark; the cheaper option wins.
docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:168:The `volatile.rs` clock read stays `std::time::SystemTime` on native targets; WASM uses a host callback (see § 3.2.1). Time-zone offset acquisition is the lone "real" external dependency — `iana_time_zone` (~50 LOC, single-purpose) vs libc FFI `localtime_r` — decision deferred to 4.5.A implementation per § 3.2.1.

docs/audits/2026-05-13-session-final-megaudit-sonnet.md:124:**W5-56 (`6c65fea5037`)** — CORRECT. Text functions use UTF-8 char-count semantics (documented divergence). FIND empty-needle returns start_num (Excel canon). SEARCH case-insensitive via `.to_uppercase()` char conversion is correct for ASCII/BMP but documented as deferred for locale-sensitive cases. REPT 32,767-char cap enforced. SUBSTITUTE empty old_text no-op is Excel canon.
docs/audits/2026-05-13-session-final-megaudit-sonnet.md:126:**W5-57 (`7a80eaf4412`)** — MOSTLY CORRECT. CEILING/FLOOR sign rules are correct. MROUND sign-mismatch caught correctly. However, MROUND(non-zero, 0) returns 0 instead of #NUM! (H1 above). ACOSH domain check `n < 1.0` is correct (ACOSH(1) = 0, valid). ATANH domain is `|n| < 1` with `±1` → `#NUM!` which matches Excel. LCM using u128 to defer overflow is sound. GCD with gcd(0,0) = 0 is correct.

docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:44:The `other =>` arm catches everything not matched above and returns `BindError::ArrayCellNotLiteral { ... got: variant_kind(other) }`. The disallowed kinds that reach this arm are: `CellRef`, `RangeRef`, `Binary`, `Unary`, `Function`, `Array` (nested), `Spill`, `NameRef`. All eight are rejected.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:145:Binder allows: `Expr::Number`, `Expr::Bool`, `Expr::String`, `Expr::Error`. Everything else → `BindError::ArrayCellNotLiteral`.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:169:    return Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:176:The parser uses `ParseError::EmptyArrayLiteral` — a **dedicated** variant with its own `#[error("empty array literal '{{}}' is not allowed")]`. The binder uses the generic `BindError::UnsupportedVariant`.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:178:**Why it matters:** Design §5 explicitly states "No `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` for `Expr::Array` itself — it's now supported." The zero-row case is a degenerate form of `Expr::Array`, not an "unsupported variant." A consumer pattern-matching on `BindError` to distinguish "array not yet supported" from "your array construction was malformed" cannot distinguish these two cases today.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:180:**Suggested fix:** Add `BindError::EmptyArrayLiteral` mirroring `ParseError::EmptyArrayLiteral`:
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:191:And replace the `UnsupportedVariant` return in the binder with `BindError::EmptyArrayLiteral`.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:193:The existing test `bind_array_literal_zero_rows_rejects` checks `matches!(err, BindError::UnsupportedVariant(_))` — it would need updating to `BindError::EmptyArrayLiteral`.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:296:        BindError::ArrayCellNotLiteral { got, .. } => assert_eq!(got, "NameRef"),
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:308:        BindError::ArrayCellNotLiteral { got, .. } => assert_eq!(got, "Function"),
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:320:This is internally consistent (the outer `ExprPlan::Array` is in a scalar argument position, so `#CALC!` per §6.3), but it is not what Excel does: `=SUM({1, #N/A, 3})` should produce `#N/A` via error propagation, not `#CALC!`. The correct path for this is the "aggregate-arg context" described in design §6.3 — which is deferred to Phase 4.7.G.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:381:Similarly, `BindError::UnsupportedVariant`'s doc comment (lines 117-120) reads:
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:443:**L-1** (`plan.rs`): Replace `BindError::UnsupportedVariant(...)` for the zero-row `Expr::Array` case with a dedicated `BindError::EmptyArrayLiteral` variant, parallel to `ParseError::EmptyArrayLiteral`. Requires: (a) adding the variant, (b) updating the binder early-return, (c) updating the test's `matches!` check. ~15 lines total.
docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-sonnet-review.md:446:1. Module-level `## Phase 0 scope` deferred list: remove `Array` from "Phase 3+ dynamic arrays"; note it's now supported.

docs/audits/2026-05-12-megaudit.md:87:A formula authored against `Sheet3!A1` in a workbook now with 2 sheets binds to `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet: 2, ... }`, which `WorkbookEnv::read_cell` maps to `Value::Blank`. The env.rs comment admits this is a deferred semantic ("Phase 3+ FormulaRegion binder"). Two agents flagged it. The same agents flagged that `Phase 2A.6` already established the pattern (`NamedTargetIsBlank`, `NamedTargetIsError`) for surfacing previously-silent paths.
docs/audits/2026-05-12-megaudit.md:89:**Fix**: at bind time, check `addr.sheet < workbook.sheet_count()` and return `BindError::InvalidSheetRef`; OR change `WorkbookEnv::read_cell` to return `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)` for missing sheets. The second is closer to Excel canon. ~1 hour.

Welford verified to ≥8 sig figs on variance and ≥12 sig figs on mean — the A6 spec target. The pre-existing `_shape_lock_offset_with_small_variance` test renamed to clarify it's a regime test (offset 10^6, step 10^-3, 1000 values, closed-form analytic moments). Literal `.dat` file import still deferred to a Phase 3+ test-asset cycle.

However, the formula text is persisted via `.qbook` save/load and op-log replay — a stale text will silently carry incorrect references into future phases when named formulas are evaluated. The comment says "today named formulas are deferred" but does not document this as a known gap or file a follow-up issue. Severity: MEDIUM (latent correctness gap, not a current user-visible crash).

docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:68:| VLOOKUP | ⚠️ | 8 | 4.3 V2 | W5-54: exact match (range_lookup=FALSE/0) and approximate match (default, sorted ascending). col_index_num<1 → #VALUE!; col_index_num>cols → #REF!; not-found → #N/A. Case-insensitive text equality. Wildcards deferred. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:108:| TRUE / FALSE | ⚠️ | — | 0 | Parsed as identifiers; promoted at bind; explicit `Bool` Token deferred to 4.1 follow-up |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:121:| CEILING | ✅ | 4 | 4.3 V2 | W5-57: round away from zero to nearest multiple of significance. Default significance=1. Excel sign rule: number > 0 with significance < 0 → #NUM!. CEILING.MATH (mode flag) deferred. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:122:| FLOOR | ✅ | 3 | 4.3 V2 | W5-57: round toward zero to nearest multiple. Same sign rule as CEILING. significance=0 with non-zero number → #DIV/0! (Excel canon — diverges from CEILING which returns 0). FLOOR.MATH deferred. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:123:| CEILING.MATH | ✅ | 7 | 4.3 polish | W5-61: signature `(number, [significance], [mode])`. Significance is `abs(significance)` — sign ignored (unlike CEILING). Default significance=1, mode=0. Positive number always rounds toward +∞; negative number with mode=0 rounds toward +∞ (toward zero), with mode≠0 rounds toward -∞ (away from zero). significance=0 returns 0. CEILING.PRECISE deferred to 4.10. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:124:| FLOOR.MATH | ✅ | 8 | 4.3 polish | W5-61: signature `(number, [significance], [mode])`. Mirror of CEILING.MATH. Positive number rounds toward -∞; negative+mode=0 toward -∞ (away from zero); negative+mode≠0 toward +∞ (toward zero). significance=0 + nonzero number → #DIV/0! (mirrors FLOOR canon, diverges from CEILING.MATH). FLOOR.PRECISE deferred to 4.10. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:156:| LEFT / RIGHT / MID | ⚠️ | 9 | 4.3 V2 | W5-56: 1-based positions; UTF-8 char-count (UTF-16 canon deferred to a future Phase, same divergence class as LEN). Negative num_chars → #VALUE!; MID start < 1 → #VALUE!. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:162:| CONCATENATE | ⚠️ | 4 | 4.3 V2 | W5-56: variadic scalar args, no range support (Excel CONCATENATE is the legacy non-range version). Errors propagate. CONCAT (range-aware variant) deferred to next batch. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:171:| TEXT | ✅ | 28 | 4.5.E | W5-83: render Value via parsed Excel format string. Built on the W5-77→W5-82 format module (parser+renderer+FormatTable+overlay+runtime). Parse failures (incl. V2-deferred [Red]/conditionals/elapsed/fraction) surface as #VALUE!. Tests in `format::text_fn::tests` + `workbook_runtime::tests::text_formula_*` + e2e `format_persistence_round_trip_through_qbook`. |
docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:199:| LOOKUP | ❌ | 0 | 4.10 | Vector & array forms; less common — defer past V1 wave 1 |

docs/audits/.codex-w5155-prompt.md:3:Audit commit `a01b40f5101` in this repo. The change wires the W5-154 `on_table_drop` hook into `WorkbookRuntime::drop_table` in `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs`. The W5-154 hook + reverse index were added in the prior commit `b83c19c7a7d` to `crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs`.
docs/audits/.codex-w5155-prompt.md:7:1. **Validate the W5-155 wiring.** Does the call to `g.on_table_drop(&canonical)` deliver what the docstring promises? The W5-154 hook docstring on `calcgraph_session.rs:947` claims: "After this hook fires, the next recompute will: 1. Find each previously-table-referencing formula in `dirty`. 2. Re-bind it (table is gone → `BindError::UnknownTable` → `Value::Error(#NAME?)`)."
docs/audits/.codex-w5155-prompt.md:9:2. **Plan cache interaction.** `PlanCacheKey` is `(text, sheet, name_gen, cell_anchor)` (see `crates/ql-exec/src/plan_cache.rs`). Note `name_gen` is `NameTable` generation, NOT `TableTable` generation. `try_recompute_with_simd_profile` (around `workbook_runtime.rs:3350`) is the recompute path — does it correctly re-bind structured-ref formulas after a `drop_table`, or does it serve a stale cached `ExprPlan::StructuredRef { resolved: <pre-drop range> }`?
docs/audits/.codex-w5155-prompt.md:11:3. **Symmetry with peers.** `rename_table`, `rename_column`, `resize_table` all call `self.plan_cache.clear()`. `drop_table` does NOT. Is this an accidental asymmetry or intentional?
docs/audits/.codex-w5155-prompt.md:17:6. **Test coverage.** Does the new test `drop_table_fires_on_table_drop_hook_and_dirties_readers` actually prove that B1 = SUM(Sales[Qty]) ends up #NAME? after drop_table + recompute_dirty? Or only that it ends up dirty?
docs/audits/.codex-w5155-prompt.md:19:7. **Stale docstrings.** Search for any docstring or comment that claims drop_table relies on `plan_cache.clear()`, or that the hook isn't wired yet (W5-154 said "wiring lands in a follow-up commit"). Are they updated?

docs/audits/.codex-4.8.G.3-megaudit-prompt.md:14:1. **W5-156 HIGH-2 mapping correctness.** workbook_runtime.rs:3266 + :2882 map BindError::UnknownTable / UnknownTableColumn to Value::Error(Name) via put_computed_at. Is this complete? Are there other bind errors that should also map to #NAME? (UnresolvedName? UnknownSheet?)? The plan.rs docstring on UnknownTable explicitly says "mapped to Value::Error(#NAME?)" — does the docstring on related variants make analogous claims?
docs/audits/.codex-4.8.G.3-megaudit-prompt.md:18:3. **W5-159 reextract correctness.** workbook_runtime.rs:2331-2415 `reextract_table_readers`. Pattern mirrors `reextract_spill_footprint_readers`. Verify:
docs/audits/.codex-4.8.G.3-megaudit-prompt.md:20:   - PlanCacheKey construction matches the existing recompute path (text, sheet, name_gen, cell_anchor with @ awareness).
docs/audits/.codex-4.8.G.3-megaudit-prompt.md:22:   - Order in resize_table: re-extract THEN hook. Is this correct, or should they swap?
docs/audits/.codex-4.8.G.3-megaudit-prompt.md:24:4. **Resize-removes-column edge case.** If resize_table drops the last column, a formula referencing that column should produce #NAME? on next recompute. Walk the path: resize mutates metadata → reextract_table_readers tries to re-bind → bind fails (UnknownTableColumn) → mark_dirty + continue. Then on_table_resize fires (no-op since the formula's deps are stale and table still exists). Then recompute_dirty visits the dirty formula → W5-156 mapping produces #NAME?. Is this path correct?
docs/audits/.codex-4.8.G.3-megaudit-prompt.md:28:6. **Cross-cutting: 4.8.G.3 wave completeness.** Design § 14 row 7c lists 5 hooks: create / rename / drop / column-rename / resize. We shipped 4 (rename, drop, column-rename, resize). on_table_create was deferred (depends on 4.8.N soft-fail). Is this defensible, or does it leave a gap?

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:312:## 2. Non-goals (deferred)
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:463:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:467:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:468:| 8 | **4.9.H** (W5-144, shipped, binder-only — no ExprPlan variant) | Binder narrows `@expr` at BIND TIME per design § 3.3 (rules 1-9). Strategy refined during implementation: NO `ExprPlan::ImplicitIntersection` variant — every reachable case collapses to an existing ExprPlan shape, so the wrapper is dropped at bind. Mapping: rule 1 (scalar) → pass-through; rule 5 (single cell / `A1:A1`) → CellRef; rules 2/3 (single col/row range with anchor in span) → narrowed CellRef; rule 4 (2-D range) → `ExprPlan::Error(#VALUE!)`; rule 6 (WholeColumn/WholeRow) → narrowed CellRef (multi-col `A:C` → `#VALUE!`); rule 7 (array literal) → top-left literal; rule 8 (function) → bind inner as-is (scalar return passes through; array return is `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit, known gap); rule 9 (StructuredRef) → bind via `resolve_structured_ref` then patch `is_this_row: true` so eval narrows to formula row (makes `@Sales[Qty]` ≡ `Sales[@Qty]`). Nested `@@expr` collapses at bind via recursion. New `BindError::ImplicitIntersectionRequiresAnchor` fires for range narrowing without `site.cell`. **AST round-trip preserved** — printer still emits `@<inner>` per W5-143; only the eval plan drops the wrapper. **Known gaps** documented for cell-boundary spill rewiring (4.9.L): rule 7 for function-returned arrays, full rule-8 narrowing of array-returning functions, named-range narrowing for `Range`-resolving names. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:472:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:474:| 14 | **4.9.N** | Polish + deferred-style edge cases |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:487:| Q5 Locale data source | Hardcoded en/de/fr in Rust. CLDR deferred to known-gap doc. Codex + Sonnet agree. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:773:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:71:ASCII-only separators. Match Excel's en-US canonical syntax (deferred locale support is Phase 4.9):
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:829:./docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:177:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:832:./docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:181:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:833:./docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:182:| 8 | **4.9.H** (W5-144, shipped, binder-only — no ExprPlan variant) | Binder narrows `@expr` at BIND TIME per design § 3.3 (rules 1-9). Strategy refined during implementation: NO `ExprPlan::ImplicitIntersection` variant — every reachable case collapses to an existing ExprPlan shape, so the wrapper is dropped at bind. Mapping: rule 1 (scalar) → pass-through; rule 5 (single cell / `A1:A1`) → CellRef; rules 2/3 (single col/row range with anchor in span) → narrowed CellRef; rule 4 (2-D range) → `ExprPlan::Error(#VALUE!)`; rule 6 (WholeColumn/WholeRow) → narrowed CellRef (multi-col `A:C` → `#VALUE!`); rule 7 (array literal) → top-left literal; rule 8 (function) → bind inner as-is (scalar return passes through; array return is `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit, known gap); rule 9 (StructuredRef) → bind via `resolve_structured_ref` then patch `is_this_row: true` so eval narrows to formula row (makes `@Sales[Qty]` ≡ `Sales[@Qty]`). Nested `@@expr` collapses at bind via recursion. New `BindError::ImplicitIntersectionRequiresAnchor` fires for range narrowing without `site.cell`. **AST round-trip preserved** — printer still emits `@<inner>` per W5-143; only the eval plan drops the wrapper. **Known gaps** documented for cell-boundary spill rewiring (4.9.L): rule 7 for function-returned arrays, full rule-8 narrowing of array-returning functions, named-range narrowing for `Range`-resolving names. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:837:./docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:186:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:858:./docs/known-gaps.md:22:| GAP-R-07 | Phase 3.2 dep extraction loses the name when a `NameRef` resolves to `NamedTarget::Constant` or `NamedTarget::Cell` — the binder substitutes the value/CellRef and the source name does not appear in `ExprPlan` (so `CalcgraphSession::name_to_formulas` does not see it). Today the `PlanCache.name_gen` counter (Phase 2B.3) invalidates ALL formulas on any name mutation, so correctness is preserved; only the per-name precision is missing. Phase 3.3 wired `on_set_name` → `name_to_formulas`, which works for AggregateNameRef; this gap covers the scalar-NameRef precision miss that Phase 3.3 explicitly did NOT address. | `ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs` module docs (§ "Still deferred"); `ql-exec/src/plan.rs::bind_with_names` scalar-NameRef branches | Engine | Engine Phase 4 (NameRef preservation in ExprPlan + binder revision) |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:873:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:423:The original draft proposed a `table_gen: u64` field in `PlanCacheKey`. Per HIGH-1 closure (§ 12.2 — formula text is rewritten on rename), `table_gen` is no longer needed for renames. It IS needed for non-rename mutations: `resize_table` (range changes; cached plans hold the OLD resolved range), `column_rename` (column index changes), `drop_table` (cached plans hold a now-invalid range). Add `table_gen` for these cases:
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:874:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:426:struct PlanCacheKey {
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:875:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:646:4. **Plan cache key extension** — `table_gen` joins `name_gen` and `sheet` in `PlanCacheKey` (only for resize/column_rename/drop; create+rename rewrite formula text so text-keyed cache misses anyway).

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6430:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:177:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6431:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:181:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6432:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:186:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6438:docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:684:| 14 | **4.8.N** (deferred) | Soft-fail error→cell-value mapping (design § 7.4): accept formula text typed BEFORE its referenced table exists; surface error cell value; re-bind on `on_table_create`. | ⏳ DEFERRED — backwards-incompatible API contract change. Phase 4.8 ships without it; current behavior is hard-reject at `set_formula`. Fresh-session work, needs explicit user sign-off on the contract change before implementation. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6469:docs/architecture/2026-05-13-format-string-grammar.md:312:| Fraction `# ?/?` | parsed + rendered | rejected | low frequency in real workbooks; defer |

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6969:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:463:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6970:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:467:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |

The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
