docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:11964:   177	| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:11968:   181	| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:11969:   182	| 8 | **4.9.H** (W5-144, shipped, binder-only — no ExprPlan variant) | Binder narrows `@expr` at BIND TIME per design § 3.3 (rules 1-9). Strategy refined during implementation: NO `ExprPlan::ImplicitIntersection` variant — every reachable case collapses to an existing ExprPlan shape, so the wrapper is dropped at bind. Mapping: rule 1 (scalar) → pass-through; rule 5 (single cell / `A1:A1`) → CellRef; rules 2/3 (single col/row range with anchor in span) → narrowed CellRef; rule 4 (2-D range) → `ExprPlan::Error(#VALUE!)`; rule 6 (WholeColumn/WholeRow) → narrowed CellRef (multi-col `A:C` → `#VALUE!`); rule 7 (array literal) → top-left literal; rule 8 (function) → bind inner as-is (scalar return passes through; array return is `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit, known gap); rule 9 (StructuredRef) → bind via `resolve_structured_ref` then patch `is_this_row: true` so eval narrows to formula row (makes `@Sales[Qty]` ≡ `Sales[@Qty]`). Nested `@@expr` collapses at bind via recursion. New `BindError::ImplicitIntersectionRequiresAnchor` fires for range narrowing without `site.cell`. **AST round-trip preserved** — printer still emits `@<inner>` per W5-143; only the eval plan drops the wrapper. **Known gaps** documented for cell-boundary spill rewiring (4.9.L): rule 7 for function-returned arrays, full rule-8 narrowing of array-returning functions, named-range narrowing for `Range`-resolving names. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:11973:   186	| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:12339:**HIGH** [workbook_runtime.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:592) lines 592-607, [plan.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:973) lines 973-997: `PlanCacheKey` is not cell-specific, but `@` range narrowing is bind-time and anchor-dependent, so two cells with the same canonical text like `@A:A` or `@A1:A10` can reuse the first cell’s narrowed `CellRef`. Fix: include the formula address in the cache key for anchor-dependent formulas, or preserve `@` to eval time so the existing formula-cell env drives narrowing; add a two-cell regression that proves the second write does not cache-hit to the first row/col plan.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:12349:**LOW** [2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:188) lines 188-189: design §5 rows 4.9.N and 4.9.O lack shipped/deferred/current-audit annotations while the earlier rows are annotated. Fix: mark 4.9.N explicitly deferred/no-op or shipped, and mark 4.9.O as this closing audit.

Fix: include the formula address in the cache key for anchor-dependent formulas, or preserve `@` to eval time so the existing formula-cell env drives narrowing; add a two-cell regression that proves the second write does not cache-hit to the first row/col plan.

Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:421:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:182:| 8 | **4.9.H** (W5-144, shipped, binder-only — no ExprPlan variant) | Binder narrows `@expr` at BIND TIME per design § 3.3 (rules 1-9). Strategy refined during implementation: NO `ExprPlan::ImplicitIntersection` variant — every reachable case collapses to an existing ExprPlan shape, so the wrapper is dropped at bind. Mapping: rule 1 (scalar) → pass-through; rule 5 (single cell / `A1:A1`) → CellRef; rules 2/3 (single col/row range with anchor in span) → narrowed CellRef; rule 4 (2-D range) → `ExprPlan::Error(#VALUE!)`; rule 6 (WholeColumn/WholeRow) → narrowed CellRef (multi-col `A:C` → `#VALUE!`); rule 7 (array literal) → top-left literal; rule 8 (function) → bind inner as-is (scalar return passes through; array return is `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit, known gap); rule 9 (StructuredRef) → bind via `resolve_structured_ref` then patch `is_this_row: true` so eval narrows to formula row (makes `@Sales[Qty]` ≡ `Sales[@Qty]`). Nested `@@expr` collapses at bind via recursion. New `BindError::ImplicitIntersectionRequiresAnchor` fires for range narrowing without `site.cell`. **AST round-trip preserved** — printer still emits `@<inner>` per W5-143; only the eval plan drops the wrapper. **Known gaps** documented for cell-boundary spill rewiring (4.9.L): rule 7 for function-returned arrays, full rule-8 narrowing of array-returning functions, named-range narrowing for `Range`-resolving names. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:425:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:186:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:427:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:188:| 14 | **4.9.N** | Polish + deferred-style edge cases |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:487:crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:1919:        // case rename is deferred to a future sub-phase.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:488:crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:2045:    /// index is design § 4.5 / sub-phase 4.8.G.3 (deferred).
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:922:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:463:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:926:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:467:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:1303:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6430:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:177:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:1304:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6431:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:181:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:1305:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6432:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:186:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |

docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:16:Audit commit `a01b40f5101` in this repo. The change wires the W5-154 `on_table_drop` hook into `WorkbookRuntime::drop_table` in `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs`. The W5-154 hook + reverse index were added in the prior commit `b83c19c7a7d` to `crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs`.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:20:1. **Validate the W5-155 wiring.** Does the call to `g.on_table_drop(&canonical)` deliver what the docstring promises? The W5-154 hook docstring on `calcgraph_session.rs:947` claims: "After this hook fires, the next recompute will: 1. Find each previously-table-referencing formula in `dirty`. 2. Re-bind it (table is gone → `BindError::UnknownTable` → `Value::Error(#NAME?)`)."
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:22:2. **Plan cache interaction.** `PlanCacheKey` is `(text, sheet, name_gen, cell_anchor)` (see `crates/ql-exec/src/plan_cache.rs`). Note `name_gen` is `NameTable` generation, NOT `TableTable` generation. `try_recompute_with_simd_profile` (around `workbook_runtime.rs:3350`) is the recompute path — does it correctly re-bind structured-ref formulas after a `drop_table`, or does it serve a stale cached `ExprPlan::StructuredRef { resolved: <pre-drop range> }`?
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:24:3. **Symmetry with peers.** `rename_table`, `rename_column`, `resize_table` all call `self.plan_cache.clear()`. `drop_table` does NOT. Is this an accidental asymmetry or intentional?
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:30:6. **Test coverage.** Does the new test `drop_table_fires_on_table_drop_hook_and_dirties_readers` actually prove that B1 = SUM(Sales[Qty]) ends up #NAME? after drop_table + recompute_dirty? Or only that it ends up dirty?
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:32:7. **Stale docstrings.** Search for any docstring or comment that claims drop_table relies on `plan_cache.clear()`, or that the hook isn't wired yet (W5-154 said "wiring lands in a follow-up commit"). Are they updated?

docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3424:  `drop_table` calls `g.on_table_drop(&canonical)`, but does not invalidate `PlanCache`. `try_recompute_with_simd_profile` keys only on `(text, sheet, NameTable generation, cell_anchor)`, so a pre-drop cached `ExprPlan::StructuredRef { resolved: <old range> }` can be reused. `schedule_dirty()` drains dirty before eval, so the dirty mark is consumed and the formula can keep reading the old cells.  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3425:  Proposed fix: add `self.plan_cache.clear()` in `drop_table`, or better include `TableTable::generation()` in `PlanCacheKey` and rely on `TableTable::remove()` bumping it. The peer clear calls make this look accidental, not intentional.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3428:  Contract violated: “`BindError::UnknownTable` → `Value::Error(#NAME?)`.”  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3430:  Proposed fix: if the intended spreadsheet behavior is `#NAME?`, map structured-ref bind failures such as `UnknownTable` / `UnknownTableColumn` during recompute to `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name)` and write it. Otherwise downgrade the docstring to “recompute reports a bind failure and leaves prior value intact.”
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3435:  Contract not tested: `drop_table_fires_on_table_drop_hook_and_dirties_readers` only proves hook count and dirty state. It does not call `recompute_dirty()` after the drop and does not assert B1 becomes `#NAME?`.  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3436:  Proposed fix: extend the test to drop, recompute, assert B1 is `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name)`, and assert no stale `30.0` remains. This test should be written with a warmed cache, which the current `set_formula` path already provides.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3441:  Stale docstring: says `WorkbookRuntime::drop_table` does not call the hook and “wiring lands in a follow-up commit.” W5-155 now wires it.  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3450:Verification run: `cargo test -p ql-exec drop_table_fires_on_table_drop_hook_and_dirties_readers` passes, but it only verifies dirtying.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3459:  `drop_table` calls `g.on_table_drop(&canonical)`, but does not invalidate `PlanCache`. `try_recompute_with_simd_profile` keys only on `(text, sheet, NameTable generation, cell_anchor)`, so a pre-drop cached `ExprPlan::StructuredRef { resolved: <old range> }` can be reused. `schedule_dirty()` drains dirty before eval, so the dirty mark is consumed and the formula can keep reading the old cells.  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3460:  Proposed fix: add `self.plan_cache.clear()` in `drop_table`, or better include `TableTable::generation()` in `PlanCacheKey` and rely on `TableTable::remove()` bumping it. The peer clear calls make this look accidental, not intentional.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3463:  Contract violated: “`BindError::UnknownTable` → `Value::Error(#NAME?)`.”  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3465:  Proposed fix: if the intended spreadsheet behavior is `#NAME?`, map structured-ref bind failures such as `UnknownTable` / `UnknownTableColumn` during recompute to `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name)` and write it. Otherwise downgrade the docstring to “recompute reports a bind failure and leaves prior value intact.”
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3470:  Contract not tested: `drop_table_fires_on_table_drop_hook_and_dirties_readers` only proves hook count and dirty state. It does not call `recompute_dirty()` after the drop and does not assert B1 becomes `#NAME?`.  
docs/audits/2026-05-15-w5155-audit-codex.md:3471:  Proposed fix: extend the test to drop, recompute, assert B1 is `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name)`, and assert no stale `30.0` remains. This test should be written with a warmed cache, which the current `set_formula` path already provides.

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:316:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:20:5. **Mutation API** — `WorkbookRuntime::create_table`, `rename_table`, `rename_column`, `resize_table`, `drop_table`. Each emits a corresponding op-log entry.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:320:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:158:5. **Tables block ALL spill anchors inside their footprint** — REVISED post-Codex (HIGH-3). Original draft allowed within-table spills if they fit; Excel canon DOES NOT allow array formulas inside Tables. `=SEQUENCE(3)` typed into ANY cell inside a table footprint produces `#SPILL!` at the anchor (even if the resulting array would fit). Scalar formulas inside tables remain allowed. Validation runs at THREE entry points (W5-124): (a) `write_spill` consults `Workbook::table_at(anchor)` before registering — blocks the "type array formula INSIDE existing table" path; (b) `create_table` walks the proposed footprint checking `Workbook::spill_anchor_at` — blocks the "create table OVER existing anchor" path; (c) `resize_table` walks NEWLY claimed cells with the same check — blocks the "grow table TO COVER existing anchor" path. Replay arms mirror (b) + (c).
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:321:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:168:Mirrors `name_to_formulas` (existing in `CalcgraphSession`). Every formula's `extract_and_register_deps` pass discovers `StructuredRef` references and registers them in `table_to_formulas: HashMap<Arc<str>, HashSet<NodeId>>`. The runtime fires `on_table_create / on_table_rename / on_column_rename / on_table_resize / on_table_drop` hooks against this index when table metadata mutates. These hooks dirty the indexed formulas + transitively propagate dirty downstream (matching the W5-91 / `on_set_name` BFS fanout pattern). Without this index, `resize_table` growth would NOT dirty formulas reading the new rows.
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:322:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:371:   - `BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc<str>)` (e.g., `#Data` on a header-only table)
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:324:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:421:The original draft proposed a `table_gen: u64` field in `PlanCacheKey`. Per HIGH-1 closure (§ 12.2 — formula text is rewritten on rename), `table_gen` is no longer needed for renames. It IS needed for non-rename mutations: `resize_table` (range changes; cached plans hold the OLD resolved range), `column_rename` (column index changes), `drop_table` (cached plans hold a now-invalid range). Add `table_gen` for these cases:
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:327:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:604:### 12.2 `rename_table` / `rename_column` — REVISED post-Codex (HIGH-1)

The core shape is visible now: resize re-extracts before the hook, and failed rebinds only dirty the node. I’m checking the storage mutation order and the plan-cache key construction against the normal recompute path before deciding whether that is a correctness bug or just stale bookkeeping.

  1556	    /// Validation (op log append is BEFORE storage mutation per the

docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:154:1. **Names + tables share a flat namespace.** A name `Sales` and a table `Sales` cannot coexist (Excel canon: defined names + table names live in one namespace). Validation hook at create-time.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:170:Mirrors `name_to_formulas` (existing in `CalcgraphSession`). Every formula's `extract_and_register_deps` pass discovers `StructuredRef` references and registers them in `table_to_formulas: HashMap<Arc<str>, HashSet<NodeId>>`. The runtime fires `on_table_create / on_table_rename / on_column_rename / on_table_resize / on_table_drop` hooks against this index when table metadata mutates. These hooks dirty the indexed formulas + transitively propagate dirty downstream (matching the W5-91 / `on_set_name` BFS fanout pattern). Without this index, `resize_table` growth would NOT dirty formulas reading the new rows.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:419:Implementation: introduce a `BindError::is_table_related(&self) -> bool` and route table-related bind errors through a soft-fail path in `set_formula`. Recompute path naturally re-binds via plan cache invalidation when the table appears, so `on_table_create` dirties the formula and the next eval succeeds.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:624:**Cells with formula text referencing tables that didn't exist at bind time** are listed in a `pending_table_refs: HashSet<(Arc<str>, NodeId)>` map (populated when bind surfaces `UnknownTable`). `on_table_create` queries this map + dirties the matching formulas so the next eval re-binds with the new table in scope.

Skipped: `on_table_create` (depends on 4.8.N soft-fail; no pending refs to discover today) + plan cache `table_gen` field (W5-160 optional polish; current `plan_cache.clear()` is correct, just coarser). 5 mutation kinds → 5 hooks; all 4 in-scope hooks shipped with unit + integration tests pinning the contract. |

Design § 7.4 wants accept-text-and-surface-error-cell-value with re-bind on `on_table_create`. Needs explicit sign-off on the contract change before implementation.

docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.8-closing-megaudit-codex.md:321:./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:168:Mirrors `name_to_formulas` (existing in `CalcgraphSession`). Every formula's `extract_and_register_deps` pass discovers `StructuredRef` references and registers them in `table_to_formulas: HashMap<Arc<str>, HashSet<NodeId>>`. The runtime fires `on_table_create / on_table_rename / on_column_rename / on_table_resize / on_table_drop` hooks against this index when table metadata mutates. These hooks dirty the indexed formulas + transitively propagate dirty downstream (matching the W5-91 / `on_set_name` BFS fanout pattern). Without this index, `resize_table` growth would NOT dirty formulas reading the new rows.

   297	    /// Phase 3.2: reverse index from canonical name → formulas that

 10257	    /// cells on the next `recompute_dirty`. W5-155 shipped (a) but not

 10892	    #[test]

 10928	    /// **W5-159 (Phase 4.8.G.3):** the correctness bug the hook +
 10929	    /// re-extract closes. After `resize_table` grows the data range,
 10930	    /// a cell write into the NEW row (outside the OLD range) must
 10931	    /// dirty the formula via the calcgraph's range stripe and
 10932	    /// `recompute_dirty` must pick it up. Pre-W5-159 the stripe
 10933	    /// stayed registered at the OLD range; the write silently missed;
 10934	    /// the formula kept its post-resize-but-pre-write value
 10935	    /// indefinitely (until something else dirtied it). The
 10936	    /// `recompute_all` path in the existing W5-122 test happened to
 10937	    /// work because full passes don't rely on stripes — so this gap
 10938	    /// only surfaces on the incremental path.
 10939	    #[test]
 10940	    fn resize_table_grow_then_recompute_dirty_picks_up_new_range_writes() {
 10941	        use crate::CalcgraphSession;
 10942	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
 10943	        let reg = default_registry();
 10944	        let mut graph = CalcgraphSession::new();
 10945	        // Initial: header at row 0, data at rows 1-2 = 10, 20 → SUM = 30.
 10946	        {
 10947	            let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut graph);
 10948	            rt.create_table("Sales", 0, 0, 0, 3, 1, true, false, vec!["Qty".into()])
 10949	                .unwrap();
 10950	            rt.set_value(0, 1, 0, Value::Number(10.0)).unwrap();
 10951	            rt.set_value(0, 2, 0, Value::Number(20.0)).unwrap();
 10952	            let v = rt.set_formula(0, 10, 0, "SUM(Sales[Qty])").unwrap();
 10953	            assert_eq!(v, Value::Number(30.0));
 10954	            let _ = rt.recompute_dirty().expect("graph attached");
 10955	        }

 11013	        wb.put(ql_types::Address::new(0, 2, 0), Value::Number(20.0));

  1716	    /// **W5-118 (Phase 4.8.H):** drop a table's metadata. Cells inside
  1717	    /// the table footprint are untouched. Formulas referencing the
  1718	    /// dropped table re-bind to `BindError::UnknownTable` on next
  1719	    /// recompute. Emits `Op::DropTable` to the attached op log (if any).
  1720	    ///
  1721	    /// **W5-155 (Phase 4.8.G.3):** also fires the calcgraph's
  1722	    /// `on_table_drop` hook (W5-154) so formulas previously
  1723	    /// referencing this table get BFS-dirty-fanned. Pre-W5-155 the
  1724	    /// drop completed without notifying the calcgraph — formulas
  1725	    /// with cached `ExprPlan::StructuredRef` plans would continue
  1726	    /// reading the (now-untyped) cell range until something else
  1727	    /// invalidated them. Post-W5-155 the hook fires:
  1728	    /// 1. `table_to_formulas[name]` enumerates the readers.
  1729	    /// 2. Each reader marked dirty + BFS-fanout (W5-91 H2 pattern).
  1730	    /// 3. Next recompute re-binds → `BindError::UnknownTable` →
  1731	    ///    `Value::Error(#NAME?)` per the existing 4.8.F binder.
  1732	    ///
  1733	    /// The hook fires AFTER the op-log append (atomic with the
  1734	    /// mutation, per W5-103) and BEFORE `tables_mut().remove`
  1735	    /// (graph fanout is read-only on workbook state; the order
  1736	    /// is irrelevant for correctness but matches the pre-existing
  1737	    /// rename/resize convention).
  1738	    pub fn drop_table(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
  1739	        let canonical = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
  1740	        if self.workbook.tables().lookup(&canonical).is_none() {
  1741	            return Err(RuntimeError::TableNotFound(name.to_owned()));
  1742	        }
  1743	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
  1744	            oplog.append(Op::DropTable {
  1745	                name: canonical.clone(),
  1746	            })?;
  1747	        }
  1748	        // **W5-155 (Phase 4.8.G.3):** dirty-fan readers BEFORE
  1749	        // removing the metadata. The hook receives the canonical
  1750	        // uppercase name; the index is keyed identically so the
  1751	        // exact-match path always hits.
  1752	        if let Some(g) = self.graph.as_deref_mut() {
  1753	            g.on_table_drop(&canonical);
  1754	        }
  1755	        let _ = self.workbook.tables_mut().remove(&canonical);
  1756	        // **W5-156 (Phase 4.8.G.3 — HIGH-1 closure):** invalidate
  1757	        // the plan cache. `PlanCacheKey` doesn't include
  1758	        // `TableTable::generation()`, so a pre-drop cached plan
  1759	        // (`ExprPlan::StructuredRef { resolved: <pre-drop range> }`)
  1760	        // would HIT on the next recompute and silently read the
  1761	        // old cells — masking the W5-154 hook's dirty fanout and
  1762	        // violating the design § 12.4 contract ("re-bind to
  1763	        // BindError::UnknownTable → emit #NAME?"). Matches the
  1764	        // brute-force pattern in `rename_table` / `rename_column`
  1765	        // / `resize_table`. A future polish (table_gen in the
  1766	        // cache key) would replace this full flush with targeted
  1767	        // invalidation.
  1768	        self.plan_cache.clear();
  1769	        Ok(())
  1770	    }

  1772	    /// **W5-119 (Phase 4.8.I):** rename a table. Per design § 12.2 + HIGH-1
  1773	    /// closure (Codex pass-1), this REWRITES STORED FORMULA TEXT — Excel
  1774	    /// canon: the next bind sees the new name and binds successfully.
  1775	    ///
  1776	    /// Process:
  1777	    /// 1. Validate: source exists; target name available (TableTable +
  1778	    ///    NameTable shared namespace); not a no-op (same canonical name).
  1779	    /// 2. Op-log append `Op::RenameTable` (before any mutation).
  1780	    /// 3. Walk every formula cell; parse the text; rewrite via
  1781	    ///    `ast::rewrite_table_ref`; if the AST changed, print it back
  1782	    ///    and emit `Op::PutFormula` + update storage.
  1783	    /// 4. Re-key the `TableTable` entry from old canonical to new
  1784	    ///    canonical; update display_name.
  1785	    ///
  1786	    /// Returns the number of formula cells whose text was rewritten.
  1787	    /// **W5-146 (Phase 4.9.K):** set the workbook's reference mode
  1788	    /// (A1 or R1C1) and append `Op::SetReferenceMode` to the op-log.
  1789	    /// Idempotent — setting to the current value is a no-op that
  1790	    /// emits NO op (keeps the log compact).
  1791	    ///
  1792	    /// Per design § 4.4 storage canon, formula text is stored in
  1793	    /// canonical A1 form regardless of `mode`; this setting only
  1794	    /// affects how the IDE renders formulas to the user and how
  1795	    /// future calls to `set_formula(text)` interpret the input
  1796	    /// reference syntax.
  1797	    pub fn set_reference_mode(
  1798	        &mut self,
  1799	        mode: ql_types::ReferenceMode,
  1800	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
  1801	        if self.workbook.reference_mode() == mode {
  1802	            return Ok(());
  1803	        }
  1804	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
  1805	            oplog.append(Op::SetReferenceMode {
  1806	                mode: ql_oplog::ReferenceModeWire::from_runtime(mode),
  1807	            })?;
  1808	        }
  1809	        self.workbook.set_reference_mode(mode);
  1810	        Ok(())
  1811	    }

  1816	    ///
  1817	    /// Per design § 4.4 storage canon, formula text is stored in
  1818	    /// EN-locale form regardless of this setting; this setting only
  1819	    /// affects how the IDE renders formulas to the user (decimal
  1820	    /// separator, argument separator, array separators) and how
  1821	    /// future calls to `set_formula(text)` interpret input
  1822	    /// glyphs.
  1823	    pub fn set_locale(&mut self, locale: ql_types::Locale) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

  1828	            oplog.append(Op::SetLocale {

  1830	            })?;
  1831	        }
  1832	        self.workbook.set_locale(locale);

  1836	    pub fn rename_table(&mut self, old_name: &str, new_name: &str) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
  1837	        let old_canonical = old_name.to_ascii_uppercase();
  1838	        let new_canonical = new_name.to_ascii_uppercase();
  1839	        if self.workbook.tables().lookup(&old_canonical).is_none() {
  1840	            return Err(RuntimeError::TableNotFound(old_name.to_owned()));
  1841	        }
  1842	        // No-op rename (same canonical) — accept silently without
  1843	        // emitting an op so the log stays compact.
  1844	        if old_canonical == new_canonical {
  1845	            return Ok(0);
  1846	        }
  1847	        // Target uniqueness.
  1848	        if self.workbook.tables().lookup(&new_canonical).is_some() {
  1849	            return Err(RuntimeError::TableCreateRejected {
  1850	                name: new_name.to_owned(),
  1851	                reason: "table with this canonical name already exists (rename target)",
  1852	            });
  1853	        }
  1854	        if self.workbook.names().lookup_ci(&new_canonical).is_some() {
  1855	            return Err(RuntimeError::TableCreateRejected {
  1856	                name: new_name.to_owned(),
  1857	                reason: "defined-name with this canonical name already exists (rename target)",
  1858	            });
  1859	        }
  1860	        // Op-log append BEFORE mutation (W5-103 atomicity). We emit
  1861	        // RenameTable + N PutFormula ops; if the log append fails,
  1862	        // no workbook state has changed yet.
  1863	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
  1864	            oplog.append(Op::RenameTable {
  1865	                old_name: old_canonical.clone(),
  1866	                new_name: new_canonical.clone(),
  1867	            })?;
  1868	        }
  1869	        // Walk formula cells and rewrite. Collect first to avoid borrow
  1870	        // conflicts (iter holds &workbook; rewrite needs &mut workbook).
  1871	        let new_display_arc: Arc<str> = Arc::from(new_name);
  1872	        let formulas: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = self
  1873	            .workbook
  1874	            .iter_formulas()
  1875	            .map(|(s, r, c, text)| (s, r, c, Arc::clone(text)))
  1876	            .collect();
  1877	        let mut rewritten = 0;
  1878	        for (s, r, c, text) in formulas {
  1879	            let tokens = match lex(text.as_ref()) {
  1880	                Ok(t) => t,
  1881	                Err(_) => continue, // malformed → leave alone (rewrite is best-effort)
  1882	            };
  1883	            let expr = match parse(tokens) {
  1884	                Ok(e) => e,
  1885	                Err(_) => continue,
  1886	            };
  1887	            let new_expr =
  1888	                ql_formula_syntax::rewrite_table_ref(&expr, &old_canonical, &new_display_arc);
  1889	            if new_expr == expr {
  1890	                continue; // no StructuredRef references the renamed table
  1891	            }
  1892	            let new_text = ql_formula_syntax::print(&new_expr);
  1893	            // Emit PutFormula so replay reconstructs the rewrite.
  1894	            if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
  1895	                oplog.append(Op::PutFormula {
  1896	                    sheet: s,
  1897	                    row: r,
  1898	                    col: c,
  1899	                    text: new_text.clone(),
  1900	                })?;
  1901	            }
  1902	            // Update workbook formula text directly (bypass set_formula
  1903	            // to avoid re-running the bind eagerly; the recompute path
  1904	            // will re-bind against the new name at next eval).
  1905	            self.workbook.put_formula(s, r, c, new_text);
  1906	            rewritten += 1;
  1907	        }
  1908	        // Re-key the TableTable entry.
  1909	        let mut meta = self
  1910	            .workbook
  1911	            .tables_mut()
  1912	            .remove(&old_canonical)
  1913	            .expect("verified at top");
  1914	        let new_canonical_arc: Arc<str> = Arc::from(new_canonical.as_str());
  1915	        meta.name = Arc::clone(&new_canonical_arc);
  1916	        meta.display_name = Arc::clone(&new_display_arc);
  1917	        self.workbook.tables_mut().insert(new_canonical_arc, meta);
  1918	        // **W5-157 (Phase 4.8.G.3):** fire the calcgraph hook to
  1919	        // re-key `table_to_formulas[OLD] → [NEW]`, substitute the
  1920	        // Arc<str> in each reader's `deps.tables` so a future
  1921	        // `remove_formula_deps` cleans up correctly, and dirty-fan
  1922	        // the readers. Without this, a subsequent `drop_table(NEW)`
  1923	        // would miss every formula that previously bound against
  1924	        // `OLD` (the index still keys them under the stale name).
  1925	        if let Some(g) = self.graph.as_deref_mut() {
  1926	            g.on_table_rename(&old_canonical, &new_canonical);
  1927	        }
  1928	        // Invalidate plan cache by clearing — every formula that
  1929	        // referenced the OLD name now has new text, so cache lookups
  1930	        // miss anyway; the bare invalidation prevents stale entries.
  1931	        self.plan_cache.clear();
  1932	        Ok(rewritten)
  1933	    }

  3828	    /// **W5-154:** `on_table_drop` is case-insensitive on lookup.
  3829	    /// Formula registers under `"Sales"` (parser case-preserves);
  3830	    /// `on_table_drop("SALES")` or `on_table_drop("sales")` still
  3831	    /// dirties it via the case-insensitive fallback scan.
  3832	    #[test]
  3833	    fn on_table_drop_is_case_insensitive_via_fallback_scan() {
  3834	        use std::sync::Arc;
  3835	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  3836	        let wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();
  3837	        let plan = ExprPlan::StructuredRef {
  3838	            table_name: Arc::from("Sales"),

  3840	                "Qty",

  3842	            resolved: ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 0, 10, 0),
  3843	            is_this_row: false,
  3844	        };
  3845	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan, &wb);
  3846	        let f1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 5, 5).unwrap();
  3847	        s.take_dirty();
  3848	
  3849	        // Lowercase name should still dirty F1.

  3852	        assert!(
  3853	            dirty.contains(&f1),
  3854	            "on_table_drop must do case-insensitive fallback when exact-match misses"
  3855	        );
  3856	    }

  3858	    /// **W5-154:** rebinding a formula whose StructuredRef target
  3859	    /// changed evicts the stale entry from `table_to_formulas`.
  3860	    /// Pre-fix, `on_table_drop("OldTable")` would dirty the formula
  3861	    /// even after rebinding away from it.
  3862	    #[test]
  3863	    fn rebind_evicts_stale_table_to_formulas_entries() {
  3864	        use std::sync::Arc;
  3865	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  3866	        let wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();
  3867	        // First plan references "Sales".
  3868	        let plan_v1 = ExprPlan::StructuredRef {
  3869	            table_name: Arc::from("Sales"),
  3870	            source: Arc::new(ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree::BareColumn(Arc::from(
  3871	                "Qty",
  3872	            ))),
  3873	            resolved: ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 0, 10, 0),
  3874	            is_this_row: false,
  3875	        };
  3876	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v1, &wb);
  3877	        // Rebind to reference "Costs" instead.
  3878	        let plan_v2 = ExprPlan::StructuredRef {
  3879	            table_name: Arc::from("Costs"),
  3880	            source: Arc::new(ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree::BareColumn(Arc::from(
  3881	                "Total",
  3882	            ))),
  3883	            resolved: ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 1, 10, 1),
  3884	            is_this_row: false,
  3885	        };
  3886	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v2, &wb);
  3887	        let f1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 5, 5).unwrap();
  3888	        s.take_dirty();
  3889	
  3890	        // Dropping "Sales" must NOT dirty F1 (it no longer references Sales).
  3891	        s.on_table_drop("Sales");
  3892	        let dirty_after_sales = s.take_dirty();
  3893	        assert!(
  3894	            !dirty_after_sales.contains(&f1),
  3895	            "stale Sales entry must be evicted on rebind to Costs"
  3896	        );
  3897	
  3898	        // Dropping "Costs" MUST dirty F1.
  3899	        s.on_table_drop("Costs");
  3900	        let dirty_after_costs = s.take_dirty();
  3901	        assert!(
  3902	            dirty_after_costs.contains(&f1),
  3903	            "Costs reference must be tracked after rebind"
  3904	        );
  3905	    }

  3907	    /// **W5-154:** `HookCounts.table_drop` bumps per invocation
  3908	    /// regardless of whether any formulas were dirty-fanned. Useful

  3910	    #[test]
  3911	    fn on_table_drop_bumps_hook_count() {

  3920	    // W5-157 (Phase 4.8.G.3) — on_table_rename hook tests.

  3922	    /// **W5-157:** `on_table_rename` re-keys `table_to_formulas` so a
  3923	    /// subsequent `on_table_drop` under the NEW name finds the
  3924	    /// dependent formulas, and dirty-fans them.
  3925	    #[test]
  3926	    fn on_table_rename_rekeys_index_and_dirties_readers() {
  3927	        use std::sync::Arc;
  3928	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  3929	        let wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();
  3930	        let plan = ExprPlan::StructuredRef {
  3931	            table_name: Arc::from("SALES"),
  3932	            source: Arc::new(ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree::BareColumn(Arc::from(
  3933	                "Qty",
  3934	            ))),
  3935	            resolved: ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 0, 10, 0),
  3936	            is_this_row: false,
  3937	        };
  3938	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan, &wb);
  3939	        let f1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 5, 5).unwrap();
  3940	        s.take_dirty();
  3941	
  3942	        s.on_table_rename("SALES", "ORDERS");
  3943	        // Drain the rename's own dirty fanout so the next take_dirty()
  3944	        // reflects only the drop's effect.
  3945	        s.take_dirty();
  3946	
  3947	        // Re-key check: dropping under the OLD name must NOT dirty F1.
  3948	        s.on_table_drop("SALES");
  3949	        let after_old_drop = s.take_dirty();
  3950	        assert!(
  3951	            !after_old_drop.contains(&f1),
  3952	            "after rename, dropping the OLD name must not dirty the reader"
  3953	        );
  3954	
  3955	        // Dropping under the NEW name MUST dirty F1.
  3956	        s.on_table_drop("ORDERS");
  3957	        let after_new_drop = s.take_dirty();
  3958	        assert!(
  3959	            after_new_drop.contains(&f1),
  3960	            "after rename, dropping the NEW name must dirty the reader"
  3961	        );
  3962	    }

docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:164:Decision: use `Arc<str>` (uppercase canonical) as the primary key, matching `NameTable`. A separate `TableId(u32)` is tempting for the graph (stable across renames) but adds complexity. **Renames REWRITE formula text** (Excel canon, see § 12.2). The plan-cache-invalidation alternative (the original draft's approach) was broken: cached plans rebind against the OLD name and fail `BindError::UnknownTable`, breaking bind/recompute/persistence. Rewrite-on-rename is the simpler correct path.
docs/architecture/2026-05-14-structured-references-and-tables.md:170:Mirrors `name_to_formulas` (existing in `CalcgraphSession`). Every formula's `extract_and_register_deps` pass discovers `StructuredRef` references and registers them in `table_to_formulas: HashMap<Arc<str>, HashSet<NodeId>>`. The runtime fires `on_table_create / on_table_rename / on_column_rename / on_table_resize / on_table_drop` hooks against this index when table metadata mutates. These hooks dirty the indexed formulas + transitively propagate dirty downstream (matching the W5-91 / `on_set_name` BFS fanout pattern). Without this index, `resize_table` growth would NOT dirty formulas reading the new rows.

Loud refusal preserves semantic integrity.

   805	/// **W5-113 (Phase 4.8.D):** apply OOXML structured-reference escapes
   806	/// to a column-name string when emitting it inside `[...]` bracket
   807	/// content. Each of `[`, `]`, `#`, `@`, `'` is preceded by `'`. The
   808	/// lexer's `consume_structured_ref_bracket` reverses this on re-lex.
   809	fn escape_for_sref(name: &str) -> String {
   810	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(name.len());
   811	    for c in name.chars() {
   812	        if matches!(c, '[' | ']' | '#' | '@' | '\'') {
   813	            out.push('\'');
   814	        }
   815	        out.push(c);
   816	    }
   817	    out
   818	}
   819	
   820	/// **W5-112 (Phase 4.8.C / 4.8.D):** print the bracket content of a
   821	/// structured reference. Column names are emitted via
   822	/// [`escape_for_sref`] so the round-trip lex(print(parse(x))) = x for
   823	/// any well-formed column name.
   824	fn print_sref_spec(spec: &crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree, out: &mut String) {
   825	    use crate::ast::{SpecialItem, TableSpecItem, TableSpecSubtree};
   826	    match spec {
   827	        TableSpecSubtree::BareColumn(name) => {
   828	            // BareColumn: name is the entire bracket content. Escape
   829	            // any special chars (lexer unescapes).
   830	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(name));
   831	        }
   832	        TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumn(name) => {
   833	            // Canonical printed form: `@[Col]` (bracketed) so a name
   834	            // starting with `[` or `#` doesn't collide with the
   835	            // unbracketed `@Col` shorthand. Always emit brackets for
   836	            // round-trip stability.
   837	            out.push('@');
   838	            out.push('[');
   839	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(name));
   840	            out.push(']');
   841	        }
   842	        TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumnRange(c1, c2) => {
   843	            out.push('@');
   844	            out.push('[');
   845	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c1));
   846	            out.push(']');
   847	            out.push(':');
   848	            out.push('[');
   849	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c2));
   850	            out.push(']');
   851	        }
   852	        TableSpecSubtree::Combination(items) => {
   853	            for (i, item) in items.iter().enumerate() {
   854	                if i > 0 {
   855	                    out.push_str(", ");
   856	                }
   857	                match item {
   858	                    TableSpecItem::Special(s) => {
   859	                        out.push('[');
   860	                        out.push_str(match s {
   861	                            SpecialItem::Headers => "#Headers",
   862	                            SpecialItem::Totals => "#Totals",
   863	                            SpecialItem::Data => "#Data",
   864	                            SpecialItem::All => "#All",
   865	                            SpecialItem::ThisRow => "#This Row",
   866	                        });
   867	                        out.push(']');
   868	                    }
   869	                    TableSpecItem::Column(c) => {
   870	                        out.push('[');
   871	                        out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c));
   872	                        out.push(']');
   873	                    }
   874	                    TableSpecItem::ColumnRange(c1, c2) => {
   875	                        out.push('[');
   876	                        out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c1));
   877	                        out.push(']');
   878	                        out.push(':');
   879	                        out.push('[');

LOW: [workbook_runtime.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:2309) still says resize cache clearing is a deferred targeted-invalidation substitute. Proposed fix: replace with “full cache flush until `table_gen` key polish; dirty propagation is handled below.”

LOW: [plan.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:265) documents `UnknownTableColumn` only as a missing-column bind error, while W5-156 now maps it to `#NAME?` in recompute paths just like `UnknownTable`. I would not add `UnresolvedName` or `UnknownSheet` to W5-156: existing recompute contracts intentionally keep general structural bind failures as `RecomputeFailure`s, and `UnknownSheet` does not have an analogous `#NAME?` mapping docstring.

LOW: [workbook_runtime.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:11071) only tests remove-last-column metadata. The graph-backed “removed referenced column becomes `#NAME?` on `recompute_dirty`” path is correct by inspection, but unpinned. Proposed fix: add a test with `SUM(Sales[Price])`, resize removing `Price`, then assert no failures and cell value `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name)` after `recompute_dirty`.

**Design Checks**

W5-157 multi-table rename is correct: `on_table_rename` gathers only dependents indexed under old `SALES`, replaces only `deps.tables` entries matching `old_name`, removes only old-name reverse-index keys, and inserts those dependents under `COSTS`. Existing `ORDERS` entries remain untouched.

W5-159 re-extract is sound: borrow phases are separated, `PlanCacheKey` matches recompute (`text`, `sheet`, `name_gen`, `cell_anchor` when text contains `@`), and re-extract-before-hook is defensible. Bind-failure leaving stale deps is acceptable here; cleaning them would lose the useful table reverse-index path that lets a later resize/add-back rebind rescue the formula.

`on_table_create` deferral is defensible because current `set_formula` hard-rejects missing tables, so there are no pending successful formula nodes for a create hook to find. The remaining gap is 4.8.N soft-fail, not this hook wave.

Verification: `cargo test -p ql-exec table_ -- --nocapture` passed.
